{{CoreHanging
**Hourglass.** A sand-glass timer. Usually 15-minute or 1-hour capacity.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Fragile. Useful for spells, alchemical procedures, or simply knowing the time underground.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Compass (Lodestone).** A simple magnetic compass floated in water or suspended on a needle. Invaluable for sea and overland navigation.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 50 gp
Traits: Works reliably in settings without competing magnetic fields. Moderate bonus to Navigation checks.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Bell (Small Alarm).** A small brass bell, used hung on strings across doorways or tripwires for intruder detection.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0.5 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 1 gp
Traits: Audible up to 60 feet when rung. Defeats simple Stealth approaches if properly positioned.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Chains (10 ft).** Iron chains for restraint, secure hanging, or securing items in place.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 10 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 5 gp
Traits: Breaking strength 500 lbs. Requires lock for secure restraint (locks separate).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Manacles (Iron).** Standard wrist-irons. Interchangeable with ankle-irons by rotation.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Standard lock (Routine lockpick). Masterwork manacles (×3 cost) have Challenging locks.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Padlock (Simple).** A keyed padlock, standard design.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 20 gp (simple) / 80 gp (good) / 200 gp (masterwork)
Traits: Simple: Routine lockpick. Good: Challenging. Masterwork: Demanding.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Book (Blank).** A bound book of 100 pages of quality paper or parchment. Used for journals, records, or reference notes.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 25 gp (paper) / 60 gp (parchment)
Traits: See Chapter 17 for specific book variants.
}}

\page

{{CoreHanging
**Bucket.** A wooden or iron bucket, for carrying water, coals, or anything else a bucket can carry. Do not underestimate a bucket.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 5 sp
Traits: Capacity 2 gallons. Can be used as an improvised helmet (Slight Protection to head at significant cost to Perception).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Shovel.** An iron-bladed shovel for digging.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 2 gp
Traits: Can be used as an improvised weapon (Solid damage). Digging speed depends on soil type; a full grave takes several hours.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Pickaxe.** An iron-headed pick for breaking stone and ice.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 7 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 4 gp
Traits: Improvised weapon (Heavy damage, Armor-Crack Slight). Essential for serious mining or quarrying.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fishing Tackle.** Line, hooks, bait, a small net. Everything needed for catch-your-own-meal.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 sp
Traits: Survival check to catch fish; modifiers depend on location and season.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Trap (Small Animal).** A spring or deadfall trap for small game. Requires placement and patience.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 5 gp
Traits: Survival check to set. A good set produces 1d3 catches per night in good country.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Snowshoes.** Rawhide-laced wooden frames strapped to the boots for walking on deep snow.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 3 gp
Traits: Negates deep-snow movement penalty. Useless on bare ground; cumbersome indoors.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Bedroll.** A wool blanket and oilcloth ground sheet, rolled together for travel.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 1 gp
Traits: Enables comfortable rest in fair weather. Cold-weather bedrolls (×4 cost) enable rest in freezing conditions.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Tent (One-Person).** A simple canvas tent for one sleeper.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 10 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 5 gp
Traits: Rain protection. Setup: 5 minutes.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Tent (Pavilion).** A large canvas pavilion, sleeps 4–6 people with gear. Used by traveling merchants and noble hunting parties.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 80 lbs · Bulk: 5
Cost: 40 gp
Traits: Requires 2 people and 30 minutes to set up. Heraldic versions with painted canvas (+Moderate cost).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Signal Horn.** A brass or bone horn for battlefield signals, hunting calls, or alarum.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 25 gp
Traits: Audible to 1,000+ feet. Different calls can signal specific orders to trained troops.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Holy Symbol.** A religious icon appropriate to a character's faith. Required in most settings for divine magic or turning undead.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5–25 gp (standard) / 200+ gp (masterwork)
Traits: Game-specific mechanics for divine characters.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Spell Component Pouch.** A compartmented pouch holding various small components — herbs, minerals, oddments — required for spellwork in systems that track material components.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 25 gp
Traits: Game-specific. Enables spellcasting with material components.
}}

\page

## Pre-Built Kits

{{CoreBody
Rather than shopping for each item separately, characters can purchase ready-packed kits. Each is a practical bundle for a specific adventuring purpose.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Explorer's Kit.** 10 ft pole, rope (50 ft), grappling hook, iron spikes (12), chalk, flint & steel, torch (2), bedroll, waterskin, trail rations (3 days), compass.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~35 lbs · Total Cost: 60 gp (Moderate cost reduction from à la carte)
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Siege-Breaker's Kit.** Sledgehammer, crowbar, pitons (10), rope (50 ft silk), climber's kit, hammer, heavy gloves, eye-guards, torch (4).
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~50 lbs · Total Cost: 150 gp
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Scholar's Kit.** Writing kit, 3 blank books, 50 feet of chalk line, spyglass, inkpot (3 colors), scroll tubes (4), reading candles (12), rubbing paper.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~15 lbs · Total Cost: 280 gp
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Thieves' Kit (Complete).** Thieves' tools, black hood, soft-soled boots, dark cloak, silent walking stick, mirror on rod, caltrops, whistle, garrote.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~10 lbs · Total Cost: 180 gp
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Dungeoneer's Kit.** Explorer's Kit + bullseye lantern + 2 flasks of oil + sun-rod + 50 extra feet of rope + climber's kit.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~55 lbs · Total Cost: 120 gp
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Hunter's Kit.** Bow, quiver, 20 broadheads, 3 days rations, fishing tackle, snares (3), skinning knife, salt (for preservation).
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Total Weight: ~20 lbs · Total Cost: 70 gp
}}

\column

## The "Breaking Things" Reference

{{CoreBody
Players will, inevitably, want to break things. Durability values for common objects follow, for quick reference.
}}

{{TableTitle
Durability of Common Objects
}}

| Object | Durability | Hardness | Notes |
|:--|:-:|:-:|:--|
| Glass bottle | 3 | 0 | Shatters |
| Ceramic jar | 6 | 0 | Breaks into shards |
| Wooden door (std) | 40 | 5 | Force attempts |
| Wooden door (heavy) | 80 | 8 | Siege-bracing |
| Barred iron door | 150 | 15 | Needs real effort |
| Chain | 80 | 10 | Per link |
| Rope | 10 | 0 | Per inch of thickness |
| Wooden chest | 60 | 5 | Drops, strikes |
| Iron-bound chest | 120 | 10 | |
| Wooden floor | 40 | 5 | Per 5 ft² |
| Stone floor | 200 | 15 | Per 5 ft² |
| Barrel (sealed) | 80 | 5 | Drops when struck |
| Iron bars (single) | 70 | 15 | Cell window |
| Masonry wall | 1,000+ | 15 | See Ch 12 |

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote
The Ultimate Provisioner's Handbook — Chapter 14: Adventuring Gear & Sundries

v0.1 working draft
}}

\page

# Chapter 15: Food, Drink & Brewing

{{Epigraph
*"An army marches on its stomach. An adventuring party forgets its stomach until the second day in, when they remember and blame each other for not packing."*
}}
{{EpigraphAuthor
— Cook Martell, formerly of the Iron Fist Company
}}

## The Provisioning Problem

{{CoreBody
Most fantasy adventuring pretends that food is either infinite or trivially procured. This chapter exists to gently push back. Food weighs something. It spoils. It varies wildly in nutrition and quality. It costs money. And in a siege, a winter, or a long sea voyage, it is often the thing that decides whether anyone goes home.
}}

{{CoreBody
The chapter covers standard rations, preservation techniques, cooking, brewing (beer, wine, mead, spirits), the rules for hunger and thirst, and the specific intoxication effects of various alcohols. It deliberately keeps the numbers simple — you do not need to track calories; you need to track whether the party has food this week.
}}

## Rations

{{CoreBody
A "daily ration" is the food a working adult consumes in one day. Quality, weight, and shelf-life vary enormously.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Trail Rations (Standard).** Hardtack biscuits, salt pork or dried beef, hard cheese, dried fruit. Shelf-stable for weeks, bland to the point of spite.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs/day · Cost: 5 sp/day · Shelf-Life: 30 days
Traits: Standard adventurer's fare. Tolerable in small quantities; dispiriting over long periods (Slight morale penalty after 14 consecutive days of trail rations alone).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Iron Rations.** Highly-preserved long-term food: cured salt meat, dense hardtack, preserved lard, pickled vegetables. Almost inedible; lasts for months.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1.5 lbs/day · Cost: 8 sp/day · Shelf-Life: 6 months
Traits: Emergency or long-voyage food only. Moderate morale penalty after 7 consecutive days. Often the last food in a besieged castle.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fresh Provisions.** Fresh bread, meat, vegetables, dairy, fruit. The food of ordinary civilian life.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2.5 lbs/day · Cost: 1 gp/day · Shelf-Life: 2–5 days
Traits: Restores morale penalties accrued from long ration diets. Cannot be transported far without spoiling.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Noble Fare.** Varied and well-prepared meals — spiced roast meats, fine bread, preserves, imported goods. The food of courts, feasts, and wealthy inns.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs/day · Cost: 5–20 gp/day · Shelf-Life: 1 day
Traits: Moderate bonus to morale and Presence-based rolls during the meal. Requires a proper setting; does not travel well.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Foraged Rations.** Hunted, fished, gathered from the wild. Quality depends on the land and the forager.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: varies · Cost: Survival check (Routine to Demanding depending on terrain)
Traits: See Foraging rules below. Fresh-food benefits; limited shelf-life.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Ship's Biscuit (Hardtack).** The baseline food of naval service. Wheat flour, salt, water, baked three or four times to remove all moisture. Lasts indefinitely. Infested with weevils almost immediately; sailors learn to eat quickly and not look closely.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb/day · Cost: 3 sp/day · Shelf-Life: Years (with weevils)
Traits: Standard fleet ration. Must be soaked or boiled before most people can chew it.
}}

## Foraging

{{CoreBody
In appropriate terrain, characters can supplement or replace purchased rations by foraging.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fertile Country (Forest, Riverside, Meadow).** Survival check at Routine difficulty per day. Success: 1d4 days of rations for the whole party. Failure: nothing, but no harm done.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Marginal Country (Mountain, Heath, Scrub).** Challenging difficulty. Success: 1d3 rations. Failure: nothing.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Hostile Country (Desert, Deep Tundra, Badlands).** Demanding difficulty. Success: 1 ration. Failure: nothing.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Hostile + Unknown (Strange Plants, Alien Terrain).** Near-Impossible difficulty with risk of poisoning.
}}

\page

## Preservation Techniques

{{CoreBody
Food preservation is a pre-industrial craft of real sophistication. The major methods:
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Salt-Curing.** Meat packed in dry salt or brine, drawing out moisture. Lasts months; dense, heavy, overwhelmingly salty. Requires large quantities of salt.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Smoking.** Meat or fish hung over a slow smoke fire for days. Preserves effectively; adds flavor; produces a distinctive smoked texture.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Drying.** Meat, fruit, or vegetables dried in sun or air. Lightweight result (a third of the fresh weight); lasts weeks to months.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Pickling.** Vegetables, eggs, or tender meats preserved in vinegar, brine, or fermented liquid. Lasts months; acquires the flavor of the pickling medium.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fermenting.** Certain vegetables (cabbage, radish) and dairy (cheese, yogurt) are preserved by beneficial fermentation. Lasts weeks to years depending on method.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Freezing (Where Available).** In cold regions, food is simply buried in snow or stored in ice-houses. Lasts through winter; spoils rapidly when removed.
}}

### Preservation Rules

{{CoreBody
Characters with a Cook skill can preserve fresh food to extend its shelf-life. Requires time (usually 1–3 days per batch), appropriate materials (salt, fuel for smoke, vinegar, etc.), and a facility.
}}

{{TableTitle
Preservation Yields
}}

| Method | Time | Ingredients | Shelf-Life |
|:--|:-:|:--|:-:|
| Salt meat | 3 days | 1 lb salt / 5 lbs meat | 2 months |
| Smoked meat | 2 days | Smokehouse, wood | 1 month |
| Dried meat | 4 days | Sun/air drying | 3 months |
| Pickled | 1 day | Vinegar, jars | 6 months |
| Jerky | 2 days | Salt + drying | 2 months |

\column

## Cooking

{{CoreBody
Cooking is treated as a skill (Chapter 3). Most meals require a Routine-to-Challenging Cook check, appropriate ingredients, a cooking surface, and time.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Camp Cooking.** Routine. Simple meals — stew, porridge, boiled grain, roasted meat. Most adventuring-party cooks operate at this level.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Proper Meals.** Challenging. Multi-course, baked bread, preserved-ingredient work. Requires a kitchen.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Feast-Tier.** Demanding. Multi-dish noble meals with specific regional techniques. Requires a facility, assistants, and hours.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Masterwork Cuisine.** Near-Impossible. Legendary meals — the sort that earn invitations to royal tables. Rare, transformative, memorable.
}}

### Cooking Bonuses

{{CoreBody
A well-prepared meal has mechanical effects beyond calories.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Camp Meal (Success).** Restores Slight fatigue. Morale bonus until next meal.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Proper Meal.** Slight morale bonus. Slight Endurance bonus until next rest.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Feast.** Moderate Endurance bonus until next day. Slight bonus to all Willpower and social checks during the meal.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**Masterwork Cuisine.** Major bonus to all physical/mental recovery for one day. Slight Presence bonus in social contexts through the following week.
}}

## Brewing

{{CoreBody
Brewing covers beer, ale, wine, mead, and distilled spirits. Each has its own timescale, facility requirements, and ingredient lists. Brewing is a craft with real economic weight — a successful brewer can sustain a village; a failed batch can ruin a household.
}}

\page

### Beer and Ale

{{CoreBody
The working drink of most of the world. Brewed from malted grain (usually barley, sometimes wheat or rye), hops (where available), water, and yeast. Fermentation takes 1–3 weeks; some styles age longer.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Common Ale.** The everyday drink. Modest alcohol content (3–5%), thick, often flavored with local herbs where hops are unavailable.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 1 sp per pint / 5 gp per small barrel (40 gal)
Brewing Time: 2 weeks
Intoxication: Slight after 2 pints; Moderate after 4; Major after 6
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Strong Ale.** A higher-alcohol brew (6–9%), aged longer. The drink of celebration and winter.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 3 sp per pint / 15 gp per barrel
Brewing Time: 4 weeks
Intoxication: Accelerated — one pint = Slight, two = Moderate, four = Major
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Dark Ale / Porter.** A heavy, malty, dark ale, often served as a meal in itself. Filling.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 sp per pint / 10 gp per barrel
Brewing Time: 3 weeks
Traits: Slight bonus to Endurance when substituted for a meal (briefly).
}}

### Wine

{{CoreHanging
**Table Wine.** The daily drink of wine-producing regions. Varies wildly in quality by region and vintage.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 3 sp per pint / 2 gp per bottle / 20 gp per cask
Brewing Time: 6 months minimum (fermentation + settling); better wines age years
Intoxication: Moderate after 2 pints; Major after 4
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fine Wine.** A quality vintage from a named estate. The nobility's everyday drink; the common people's luxury.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 gp per bottle / 80 gp per cask
Brewing Time: Years of aging
Traits: Slight Presence bonus when served at feasts.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Legendary Vintage.** Old-vintage wine from a famous estate in an exceptional year. A social weapon as much as a drink.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 50+ gp per bottle / 2,000+ gp per cask
Traits: Moderate Presence bonus when served. Known to connoisseurs; can be identified by name.
}}

### Mead

{{CoreHanging
**Standard Mead.** Fermented honey, water, and yeast. The drink of Northern Europe before hops spread; revered in many traditions.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 4 sp per pint / 25 gp per cask
Brewing Time: 2 months minimum
Intoxication: Moderate after 2 pints. Distinctive sweetness masks alcohol; a common hazard.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Spiced / Flavored Mead.** Mead aged with fruit, herbs, or spices. Regional traditions vary widely.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 8 sp per pint / 50 gp per cask
Brewing Time: 3–6 months
}}

### Distilled Spirits

{{CoreBody
Distillation concentrates the alcohol from fermented liquids, producing stronger drinks. The technology is later than simple brewing and requires specific equipment (stills) and training.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Common Spirit (Grain, Potato, or Fruit).** Whiskey, vodka, brandy, applejack. Regional character varies; alcohol content is uniform-to-high (30–50%).
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 gp per pint / 20 gp per bottle
Distillation Time: 1 week after fermentation (plus the weeks of fermentation beforehand)
Intoxication: Major after 2 shots (2 oz each); Severe after 4
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Aged Spirit.** Distilled spirit aged in wooden casks for years, acquiring color, smoothness, and complexity.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 20 gp per bottle / 500+ gp per cask
Aging: 3–12 years or more
Traits: Presence bonus when served. Recognizable by connoisseurs.
}}

\page

{{CoreHanging
**Absinthe / Herbal Distillate.** Distilled spirit flavored with strong herbs (wormwood, anise, etc.). Often culturally specific; sometimes considered medicinal.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 8 gp per bottle
Traits: Peculiar intoxication effects (Slight Willpower penalty beyond standard alcohol effects). May be illegal in some settings.
}}

### Brewing Economics

{{CoreBody
Brewing follows Chapter 3's crafting rules, with specific adjustments for time and facility:
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Fermentation Time.** Active labor is 2–5 days; calendar time is weeks to months. A brewer may have multiple batches in process simultaneously, rotating through fermentation tanks.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Facility.** A proper brewery includes mash tuns, fermentation vessels, storage cellars, and ideally a source of clean water. A village brewer may work from a single barrel; a commercial brewer from dozens.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Yield.** A batch produces significant quantities by adventurer standards. A small brewer's month of fermentation might yield 200 gallons — enough to supply a village tavern for weeks.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Market.** Beer and ale are the most broadly saleable brews; wine requires appropriate culture; spirits require wealthier buyers. Tavern sales make up the bulk of most brewers' income.
}}

## Intoxication

{{TableTitle
Intoxication Effects
}}

| Level | Effect |
|:--|:--|
| Slight | Moderate Willpower penalty to resist temptation. No physical effect. |
| Moderate | Slight Agility penalty; Slight Perception penalty. Socially louder. |
| Major | Moderate Agility penalty; Moderate Perception penalty. Combat-relevant impairment. |
| Severe | Major penalties to all physical rolls; speech impairment; risk of unconsciousness. |
| Critical | Unconscious. Possible alcohol poisoning (Endurance check at Demanding difficulty). |

\column

### Hangover Rules

{{CoreBody
Character who reached Major or worse intoxication suffers hangover the following day.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**After Major.** Slight penalty to all rolls until a meal and rest.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**After Severe.** Moderate penalty for one full day. Recovery requires rest, food, and water.
}}
{{CoreHanging
**After Critical.** Major penalty for 1–3 days. Full recovery assumes no further alcohol; otherwise the penalty compounds.
}}

## Hunger, Thirst, and Malnutrition

{{CoreBody
Characters who go without food or water accrue penalties per Chapter 8. The timeline:
}}

{{TableTitle
Deprivation Effects
}}

| Time Without | Food | Water |
|:--|:--|:--|
| 24 hrs | Slight hunger | Slight thirst |
| 48 hrs | Slight penalty | Moderate penalty |
| 72 hrs | Moderate penalty | Effectively incapacitated |
| 7 days | Major penalty; dying soon | Death likely within 24 hrs |
| 14 days | Death probable | — |

{{CoreBody
Malnutrition — eating only a single type of food for extended periods — is its own problem. Scurvy from lack of fresh produce at sea is the classic example. Moderate Endurance penalty after 4 weeks of monotonous iron rations; Major after 8 weeks. Recovery requires fresh food, not merely adequate food.
}}

## Regional Cuisines (Brief)

{{CoreBody
Specific cuisines produce specific dishes and flavors. The book does not attempt to enumerate these — each setting has its own culinary geography. For mechanical purposes, any regional cuisine acts like the ration-tier appropriate to its ingredients and labor (camp meal, proper meal, feast).
}}

{{CoreBody
A GM introducing a regional cuisine should note two or three signature dishes, their rough cost, and any cultural weight (the dish is associated with royalty; the dish is taboo; the dish is the traditional gift at a wedding; and so on). That level of detail is almost always sufficient for play.
}}

\page

## Summary Tables

{{TableTitle
Rations at a Glance
}}

| Ration | Weight/day | Cost/day | Shelf-Life |
|:--|:-:|:-:|:-:|
| Trail rations | 2 lbs | 5 sp | 30 days |
| Iron rations | 1.5 lbs | 8 sp | 6 months |
| Fresh provisions | 2.5 lbs | 1 gp | 2–5 days |
| Noble fare | 3 lbs | 5–20 gp | 1 day |
| Ship's biscuit | 1 lb | 3 sp | Years |

{{TableTitle
Drinks at a Glance
}}

| Drink | Cost (pint) | Cost (barrel) | Intox Rate |
|:--|:-:|:-:|:--|
| Common ale | 1 sp | 5 gp | Slight per 2 pints |
| Strong ale | 3 sp | 15 gp | Slight per pint |
| Dark ale | 2 sp | 10 gp | Slight per 2 pints |
| Table wine | 3 sp | 20 gp | Moderate per 2 pints |
| Fine wine | 2 gp/bottle | 80 gp | Moderate per 2 pints |
| Legendary vintage | 50+ gp/bottle | 2,000+ | — |
| Standard mead | 4 sp | 25 gp | Moderate per 2 pints |
| Spiced mead | 8 sp | 50 gp | Moderate per 2 pints |
| Common spirit | 2 gp | 20 gp/bottle | Major per 2 shots |
| Aged spirit | 20 gp/bottle | — | Major per 2 shots |

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote
The Ultimate Provisioner's Handbook — Chapter 15: Food, Drink & Brewing

v0.1 working draft
}}

\page

# Chapter 16: Alchemy & Consumables

{{Epigraph
*"The first rule of alchemy: keep the lid on. The second rule: know why you want to open the lid. The third rule, which bears repeating: keep the lid on."*
}}
{{EpigraphAuthor
— Master Alchemist Barbara of the Iron Flask
}}

## Alchemy as a Craft

{{CoreBody
Alchemy is the applied chemistry of a pre-industrial world. It produces consumable goods — liquids, powders, pastes, crystals — that act on the body, on the mind, or on the physical environment. Some alchemy is magical in origin; most is simply sophisticated practical chemistry elevated into a specialist profession.
}}

{{CoreBody
Alchemical items are produced by the Chapter 3 crafting framework. The alchemist adds an extra step to the process: after the labor period, an **instability check** determines whether the finished product works as intended. A failed instability check produces a dud, a delayed-reaction hazard, or occasionally an entirely different substance.
}}

### The Instability Check

{{TableTitle
Alchemical Instability Outcomes
}}

| Roll | Outcome |
|:--|:--|
| Critical success | Masterwork: extended duration / enhanced effect |
| Success | Works as intended |
| Partial | Reduced duration or effect (Slight penalty) |
| Failure | Dud: no effect |
| Critical failure | Catastrophe: item affects wrong target, detonates prematurely, or transforms unexpectedly |

{{CoreBody
Apprentice alchemists produce a significant fraction of duds; master alchemists rarely fail. The instability check uses the alchemist's relevant skill, at difficulty appropriate to the item's tier.
}}

## Light and Fire

{{CoreHanging
**Alchemist's Fire.** A thick, sticky, self-igniting oil. Thrown as a flask, it ignites on contact with air and clings to any surface. Burns vigorously for several rounds.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Damage: Heavy fire (impact) + Heavy fire per round for 3 rounds
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 50 gp per flask
Range: Thrown (30 ft)
Traits: Cannot be extinguished by water; must be smothered or burn out. Concerns: safety of the wielder on a broken flask.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Sun-Rod.** A glass tube of alchemical compound. When cracked, emits bright white light for 6 hours. Waterproof; functions underwater.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Duration: 6 hours
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 25 gp
Traits: Cannot be extinguished once activated. Produces no heat, no flame. Safe to carry in gunpowder magazines.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Tindertwig.** A small splint with an alchemical head; ignites into flame when struck sharply. Used to light lamps, fuses, and campfires without flint and steel.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 1 gp each
Traits: One-shot; ignites on Routine strike. Much faster than flint-and-steel. Works in conditions where flint-and-steel fails.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Smokestick.** A wooden shaft with an alchemical head; produces dense, opaque smoke for several rounds when broken. Used to obscure, distract, or escape.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Duration: 3 rounds · Smoke Radius: 20 ft
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 20 gp
Traits: Heavy obscurement within smoke cloud; everyone in or looking through takes Moderate Perception penalty.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Flash-Powder Bomb.** A small ceramic flask of flash powder that explodes in a brilliant white flash when thrown. Non-lethal; blinds and disorients.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Duration: 1 round blindness
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 45 gp
Range: Thrown (30 ft)
Traits: All creatures within 10 ft of impact must Save vs. blindness (Willpower, Challenging difficulty); failure imposes Major penalty for 1 round.
}}

\page

## Medicine and Healing

{{CoreHanging
**Healing Salve.** A thick paste of herbs, fats, and alchemical binders, applied to wounds. Accelerates natural recovery.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Restores a Slight amount of Durability / HP when applied; halves recovery time of a specific wound.
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 40 gp per jar (3 applications)
Traits: Not magical healing — accelerates natural processes. Requires time to act (1 day for full benefit).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Potion of Healing (Minor).** A magical liquid that restores wounds immediately when consumed. Setting-dependent magical effect.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Restores Moderate amount of Durability / HP immediately.
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 250 gp
Traits: Consumed as an action. Magical; setting-specific production rules.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Potion of Healing (Major).** A stronger healing potion, restoring serious wounds.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Restores Major amount of Durability / HP immediately.
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 750 gp
Traits: Magical production; rare.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Antitoxin.** A generic poison antidote. Slows or neutralizes various common poisons.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Grants Moderate bonus to resist poison for 1 hour, or neutralizes one ongoing minor poison effect.
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 50 gp
Traits: Not specific to any one poison; more effective against common venoms than obscure magical poisons.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Cure-Light (Herbal Tea).** A brewed concoction that mitigates fevers, restores minor energy, reduces coughing. Common at inns and in village stores.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Restores Slight fatigue; eases minor illness.
Weight: 0 lb (dose) · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 sp
Traits: No combat effect. Useful for traveling parties during epidemic seasons.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Restorative (Major).** A tonic that counteracts fatigue, exhaustion, or hunger — at significant cost.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Removes all fatigue and temporary penalties; restores Moderate Endurance.
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 150 gp
Traits: Single-use. Consumer feels normal for several hours, then potentially crashes harder (GM discretion).
}}

## Poisons

{{CoreBody
Poisons are classified by delivery: contact (absorbed through skin), ingested (consumed orally), inhaled (breathed in), and weapon-coat (applied to a blade or arrowhead). Each has its own mechanics and social context.
}}

{{CoreBody
The book notes that poison use is socially fraught in most settings. Open poison use is often considered dishonorable or illegal; assassination specialists work in the shadows accordingly. GMs should consider the narrative weight of poison use carefully.
}}

### Contact Poisons

{{CoreHanging
**Blue Whinnis.** A common plant-based contact poison. Causes unconsciousness on contact.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Onset: 1 minute · Duration: 1 hour
Effect: Save vs. Poison (Challenging) or fall unconscious.
Cost: 120 gp per dose
Traits: Applied to weapon surface or doorknob. Can affect the wielder if the wielder is careless.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Sassone Leaf Residue.** A paralytic contact poison, applied to edges or weapons to disable without killing.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Onset: 2 minutes · Duration: 30 min paralysis
Effect: Save vs. Poison (Challenging) or lose all physical action for 30 minutes.
Cost: 300 gp per dose
Traits: Valued by slavers and kidnappers.
}}

\page

### Ingested Poisons

{{CoreHanging
**Oil of Taggit.** A tasteless, odorless ingested poison; induces deep sleep.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Onset: 10 minutes · Duration: 1d4+4 hours sleep
Effect: Save vs. Poison (Challenging) or fall into unnatural sleep.
Cost: 80 gp per dose
Traits: Commonly used in tavern drinks for robbery or abduction.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Purple Worm Poison.** A slow-acting ingested poison causing serious internal damage.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Onset: 1 hour · Duration: 24 hours
Effect: Save vs. Poison (Demanding) or take Major damage + Major Endurance penalty for one day.
Cost: 700 gp per dose
Traits: Often associated with specific assassins' guilds.
}}

### Inhaled Poisons

{{CoreHanging
**Burnt Othur Fumes.** A powder thrown into a fire that produces a poisonous smoke.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Area: 10-ft radius around fire · Onset: Immediate
Effect: All within area Save vs. Poison (Challenging) each round of exposure; failure: Moderate damage and Slight Endurance penalty for 1 day.
Cost: 180 gp per dose
Traits: Indiscriminate; hurts everyone in area including thrower. Used in smoke-out raids.
}}

### Weapon-Coat Poisons

{{CoreHanging
**Giant Wasp Venom.** A venom applied to blade edges or arrowheads. Causes sharp pain and weakness.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect on hit: Save vs. Poison (Challenging) or take Moderate damage + Slight penalty for 1 scene.
Duration of coating: Next 3 hits or 1 hour.
Cost: 250 gp per dose
Traits: Can be applied to up to 3 arrows or one melee weapon.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Black Adder Venom.** A fast-acting weapon poison from the black adder serpent.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect on hit: Save vs. Poison (Demanding) or take Major damage + Slight Endurance penalty.
Duration of coating: Next 1 hit or 1 hour.
Cost: 120 gp per dose
Traits: Potent but fragile; a single application coats one strike.
}}

### The Ethics and Risks

{{CoreBody
Poison handling is inherently risky. Fumbling with a coated weapon or an open flask of contact poison requires a Routine Agility check each time; failure means the character has poisoned themselves.
}}

{{CoreBody
In most settings, open poison use is socially unacceptable among honorable combatants, but perfectly ordinary among spies, assassins, and certain specialist soldiers. GMs may impose reputation consequences for poison use observed in public.
}}

## Truth and Sleep Draughts

{{CoreHanging
**Truth-Draught.** An alchemical compound that weakens the subject's resistance to direct questioning. Not infallible; a strong-willed subject can resist or lie creatively.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Save vs. Willpower (Challenging); on failure, subject answers direct questions honestly for 1 hour.
Cost: 200 gp per dose
Traits: Illegal in most civilized jurisdictions. Detectable by some magical sight.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Sleeping Draught (Strong).** A fast-acting sleep compound, usually slipped into a drink or meal.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Save vs. Willpower (Challenging); on failure, subject falls asleep within 5 minutes for 1d6 hours.
Cost: 90 gp per dose
Traits: Common in kidnapping and theft operations. Tastes mildly bitter; a cautious drinker may notice.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Dreamer's Tincture.** A mild hallucinogenic alchemy, used recreationally and sometimes medically. Produces vivid dreams and visions.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: 4 hours of hallucinations and distorted perception; Slight Willpower penalty while active.
Cost: 40 gp per dose
Traits: Not widely illegal but socially suspect. Psychological effects vary by user.
}}

\page

## Acids and Corrosives

{{CoreHanging
**Acid Flask.** A ceramic flask of strong acid — usually refined from vitriol or nitric acid. Thrown as a missile; eats flesh, armor, or stone given time.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Damage: Heavy (acid) on hit; Slight continuing acid per round for 2 rounds
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 30 gp per flask
Range: Thrown (30 ft)
Traits: Dissolves organic material faster than metal. Can be used to dissolve ropes, doors, or small locks over minutes.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Universal Solvent.** A specialty alchemical solvent capable of dissolving seals, glues, or magical bindings. Used for safe-cracking and document forgery.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Dissolves non-metal seals, removes glue, breaks adhesive magical wards (Slight effect).
Cost: 150 gp per vial
Traits: Rare; requires specialist alchemist. Controlled substance in some jurisdictions.
}}

## Holy and Sacred

{{CoreHanging
**Holy Water.** Water consecrated by appropriate ritual. Reacts damagingly against undead and infernal entities; symbolically important in most settings.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Damage on contact: Heavy (against undead/infernals only)
Weight: 1 lb per flask · Bulk: 0
Cost: 25 gp per flask
Range: Thrown (30 ft) · Traits: No effect on living non-infernal targets. Purifies small quantities of other water. Setting-specific effects.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Sacred Oils (Anointing).** Consecrated oils used for rites, blessings, or ceremonial anointment. Various religious traditions produce their own.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Ceremonial; minor blessing effects per setting.
Cost: 30 gp per small vial
Traits: Used by clergy, paladins, and religious participants.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Incense (Ceremonial).** Rare incense used in rites of invocation or warding. Setting-dependent effects.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Duration: 1 hour of burning
Cost: 20 gp per stick
Traits: Often tied to specific rituals. GM discretion for effects.
}}

## Armor and Weapon Oils

{{CoreHanging
**Weapon Oil (Standard).** A light oil for blade maintenance, applied after cleaning to prevent rust. One flask lasts a year of ordinary use.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 2 gp per flask
Traits: Practical necessity. Not magical.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Oil of Sharpness.** An alchemical treatment that temporarily enhances a blade's cutting edge.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Applied to a weapon; grants Slight damage bonus for 1 hour or 20 strikes, whichever comes first.
Cost: 120 gp per flask
Traits: Must be applied carefully (1 minute). Does not stack with masterwork benefits.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Armor Oil (Alchemical).** A treatment for metal armor that resists corrosion in harsh environments. Especially useful for sea travel or volcanic zones.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Prevents Durability loss from environmental corrosion for 1 week per application.
Cost: 20 gp per application
Traits: Application takes 1 hour. Essential for ships' crews and mountain expeditions.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Rust Remover.** A strong alkaline solution that removes surface rust from metal without damaging underlying metal.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Restores Slight Durability to rust-damaged metal gear.
Cost: 8 gp per flask
Traits: Does not repair true corrosion damage; only cleans surface rust.
}}

\page

## Elemental Flasks

{{CoreHanging
**Flask of Frost.** An alchemical cold-compound that flash-freezes its target area when a thrown flask breaks.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Damage: Heavy cold + ground icy (Moderate Agility penalty for 1 scene)
Range: Thrown (30 ft) · Cost: 80 gp
Traits: Creates patch of ice 5-foot radius. Opponents in area take cold damage and risk falling.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Flask of Lightning (Thunder Flask).** An alchemical capacitor — a sealed jar that produces a small electrical discharge on impact.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Damage: Heavy lightning; all creatures adjacent to target also save vs. Stun (Challenging) or lose 1 action.
Range: Thrown (30 ft) · Cost: 120 gp
Traits: Specific setting requirement. Moderate chance of detonating in water.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Flask of Sulfur Cloud.** An alchemical reagent that produces toxic yellow smoke on breakage.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Area: 15-ft radius · Duration: 3 rounds · Effect: Save vs. Poison (Challenging) or lose 1 action per round in cloud
Cost: 60 gp
Traits: Area-denial weapon. Dangerous to allies as much as enemies.
}}

## Miscellaneous Consumables

{{CoreHanging
**Glowstone.** A small alchemical stone that emits low continuous light when exposed to air. Used as a long-lasting lamp substitute.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Duration: 1 week of continuous soft light
Weight: 0 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 60 gp
Traits: Dim radius 10 ft. Cannot be extinguished once exposed.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Stick-Wax.** An alchemical putty that bonds strongly to surfaces, used as a temporary adhesive or as a silent substitute for nails.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Adheres 1 lb per square inch for 1 hour; sets rock-hard after 1 day.
Cost: 5 gp per stick
Traits: Many uses — temporarily sealing a door closed, rigging a trap, repairing a crack in a barrel.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Silver Dust.** A pouch of finely-divided silver, thrown to disrupt supernatural beings sensitive to silver.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Effect: Creates a Silver-aspected zone (5-ft radius) for 3 rounds; affects lycanthropes and certain fae/undead.
Cost: 80 gp per pouch
Traits: Single use. Does not stack with silver weapons already carrying silver properties.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Salt (Blessed or Warding).** Ordinary salt consecrated for specific purposes — banishing minor spirits, warding doorways, disinfecting wounds.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 gp per pouch (blessed) / 2 sp per pouch (common)
Traits: Blessed salt repels minor undead. Common salt is a preservative and seasoning.
}}

## Crafting Alchemy

{{CoreBody
Alchemical crafting follows Chapter 3's general rules with additions:
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Facility.** A proper laboratory — ventilated workbench, range of containers, water source, heat source, safe storage for reagents. A portable alchemy kit (Chapter 14) allows limited field work but imposes a Moderate penalty on the check.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Ingredients.** Most alchemy requires specific raw materials. Common (salt, oil, plant extracts) are cheap and available; rare (dragon-gland, starcrab-shell, volcanic ash from a specific vent) are expensive and require sourcing quests.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Instability Check.** After the labor period, roll the alchemist's Alchemy check against the item's difficulty. Outcomes as per the Instability Table. A critical failure may blow up the workshop; a critical success may produce a masterwork version of the item.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Storage.** Many alchemical items have limited shelf-life. Most entries last at least a year; some (volatile or biological) expire within weeks. A cool cellar extends shelf life significantly.
}}

\page

\page

# Chapter 17: Clothing, Containers & Lifestyle Goods

{{Epigraph
*"A man is not what he owns. But what he owns, wears, and carries tells his neighbors exactly what kind of man to treat him as."*
}}
{{EpigraphAuthor
— Tailor Hesperine of the Guildhouse Spoke
}}

## Why Lifestyle Matters

{{CoreBody
Clothing is not armor; a scroll case is not a weapon; a lute is not a combat tool. But the right clothing gains a character entry to the right rooms. A seal ring conveys authority the bearer may not actually have. A quality saddle makes a week of hard riding bearable. And a lute, wielded by a competent hand, occasionally accomplishes things a sword does not.
}}

{{CoreBody
This chapter covers the goods that define social presentation, the items that store value, the instruments of civil and religious life, and the small luxuries that separate a miserable traveler from a comfortable one.
}}

## Clothing by Social Class

{{CoreHanging
**Peasant Dress (Common).** Rough wool, linen, or hemp — plain, functional, durable. The everyday dress of the working poor.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 3 sp per complete outfit
Traits: Unremarkable. Wears for years with basic care.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Traveler's Clothes.** Sturdy woolens, a cloak, a hat, and good boots. The adventurer's standard — practical, anonymous, neither impressive nor embarrassing.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 8 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 3 gp
Traits: Suitable across a range of climates and social contexts. Not noble, not peasant.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Tradesman's Dress.** The clothing of a shopkeeper, artisan, or minor professional. Better-cut cloth, a shop apron, good boots. Signals respectability.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 6 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Slight Presence bonus in urban contexts; marks the wearer as a legitimate member of the trades.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Merchant's Dress.** Fine wools or lighter silks in good cut, belt pouches, a good hat, well-made boots. The clothing of a successful trader.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 8 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 80 gp
Traits: Moderate Presence bonus in commercial contexts. Appropriate for most social settings short of a royal audience.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Noble's Everyday Wear.** Fine cloth, tailored cut, embellishments (embroidery, buttons, trim). Daily noble attire.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 10 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 300 gp
Traits: Moderate Presence bonus in most social contexts. A noble in traveler's clothes is slumming; a commoner in noble clothes is committing an offense.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Court Dress (Formal).** The full regalia of a noble court — elaborate embroidery, fur trim, specific pieces by season and rank, often fitted specifically for that court's customs.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 15 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 1,500 gp
Traits: Major Presence bonus at the court it is fitted for. Severe penalty to any physical activity. Cannot be worn on a journey; must be packed and carried.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Ceremonial / Royal Dress.** The ultimate courtly attire, often jewel-encrusted, with specific religious or political significance.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 20+ lbs · Bulk: 4
Cost: 5,000+ gp
Traits: Major Presence bonus in any formal setting. Almost impossible to move in. Identifies the wearer unmistakably.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Disguise Outfit.** A specifically-constructed outfit designed to pass as a member of a target class (beggar, courier, priest, noble, etc.).
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 15 gp (rough) / 100 gp (convincing) / 500 gp (court-passing)
Traits: Moderate bonus to Disguise for impersonating the target social role.
}}

\page

## Climate Gear

{{CoreHanging
**Winter Cloak (Heavy).** A thick wool cloak lined with fur or heavy flannel. Essential for travel in cold regions.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 6 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 20 gp
Traits: Negates Slight cold damage for ordinary winter conditions. Moderate bonus for severe cold.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Desert Robes.** Light, loose white cloth covering most of the body, with a headwrap. Protects from sun without trapping heat.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Moderate bonus to Endurance in desert conditions. Major Stealth penalty (bright white).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Oilskin Cloak.** A cloak treated with oil to shed water. The standard rain gear.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 12 gp
Traits: Keeps wearer dry in ordinary rain. Slight mildew resistance; needs annual re-oiling.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Boots, Heavy (Winter).** Fur-lined leather boots for deep cold and snow.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 4 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Negates cold-feet effects. Cannot be run in comfortably (Slight penalty when moving fast).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Boots, Light (Travel).** Standard travel boots; soft leather, well-made.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 gp
Traits: Comfortable for long walks. Wears out after a year or two of hard road use.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Boots, Riding.** Knee-high leather boots for horseback.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 12 gp
Traits: Slight bonus to Riding checks. Awkward to walk in for extended distances.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Mountain Gear (Complete).** Heavy cloak, insulated boots, face cover, altitude-appropriate layers.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 15 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 60 gp
Traits: Enables survival at altitude and in alpine conditions. Moderate Agility penalty at lower elevations.
}}

## Jewelry and Precious Items

{{CoreHanging
**Simple Ring.** A plain gold, silver, or copper band.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 gp (copper) / 20 gp (silver) / 80 gp (gold)
Traits: Wearing a ring is socially meaningful in most cultures — wedding, guild membership, etc.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Signet Ring.** A ring bearing a seal, used to authenticate documents with an impressed wax stamp.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 80 gp (silver) / 300 gp (gold) / 2,000 gp (heraldic noble)
Traits: Socially significant. A forged signet is a serious crime; a stolen one is often the subject of a quest.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Necklace / Pendant.** Ornamental; sometimes functional (reliquary, locket for miniature portrait).
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 50 gp (simple) to 10,000+ gp (famous piece)
Traits: Various social meanings.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Brooch (Cloak Clasp).** An ornamental pin used to fasten a cloak at the shoulder.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 40 gp (ordinary) to 2,000 gp (masterwork)
Traits: Identifies cultural or family affiliation in cultures that use them.
}}

\page

## Containers and Storage

{{CoreHanging
**Strongbox.** A heavy iron-bound chest with a quality lock. Used for treasures and important documents.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Capacity: 2 cubic feet · Weight: 50 lbs
Cost: 80 gp
Traits: Challenging lock. Reinforced against forced entry.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Scroll Tube.** A cylindrical case for carrying and protecting a rolled document or scroll.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 3 gp (wood) / 25 gp (metal) / 50 gp (enchanted for water-tight)
Traits: Protects scrolls from water, wear, and general damage.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Map Case.** A similar but larger tube for maps and charts.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 8 gp
Traits: Waterproof with oil treatment. Essential for navigators and scouts.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Reliquary.** A small ornate container for religious relics or sacred objects.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 50 gp (simple) to 1,000+ gp (gem-encrusted)
Traits: Culturally significant. Often a family or temple heirloom.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Trunk (Traveling).** A large wooden trunk for personal belongings, with a simple lock.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Capacity: 8 cubic feet · Weight: 60 lbs
Cost: 25 gp
Traits: Too heavy for a single person to carry comfortably. Ships aboard caravans and wagons.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Secret Compartment Insert.** A small concealed chamber fitted into a chest, wall, or piece of furniture.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 50 gp (simple) / 200 gp (cleverly hidden)
Traits: Finding a secret compartment is a Perception check at Challenging-to-Demanding difficulty depending on workmanship.
}}

\column

## Writing and Office Supplies

{{CoreHanging
**Parchment (Quality).** Sheet of cured animal skin, the durable writing surface.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 sp per sheet
Traits: Lasts centuries if protected. The standard for formal documents.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Paper.** Pressed cellulose, cheaper than parchment but less durable.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 4 cp per sheet
Traits: The common writing surface. Deteriorates in damp conditions.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Inkpot (Small).** A bottle of ink, lasting weeks to months of regular writing.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 8 sp per pot (black) / 2 gp per pot (colored)
Traits: Can be mixed to produce specific shades. Dries up if left uncovered.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Quill (Set of 5).** Shaped feather quills for writing. Require occasional re-trimming.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 1 sp per set
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Book (Blank, Fine).** A bound book with quality paper or parchment pages, good binding.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 60 gp (paper) / 200 gp (parchment)
Traits: For serious records, journals, spellbooks, or grimoires.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Book (Printed).** A printed book in settings with printing. Cheaper than a hand-copied book; mass-producible.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 15 gp (common printing) to 100 gp (illustrated)
Traits: Available only in settings with developed printing presses.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Sealing Wax (Stick).** Wax for sealing documents with a signet impression.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 1 gp per stick
Traits: Standard in all literate cultures. Red wax for nobility; other colors for specific guilds and temples.
}}

\page

## Religious Items

{{CoreHanging
**Prayer Beads / Rosary.** A string of beads used in ritual prayer.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 gp (wood) / 20 gp (bone) / 200 gp (gemstone)
Traits: Religious significance only; no mechanical effect unless setting specifies.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Altar (Portable).** A small folding altar for traveling priests.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 15 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 40 gp
Traits: Required for some divine rites away from a temple. Includes basic sacramental items.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Incense Burner.** A small metal or ceramic burner for ritual incense.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 8 gp (simple) / 50 gp (ornate)
Traits: Used in most religious traditions.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Prayer Book / Scripture.** A bound copy of a religion's sacred text.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 50 gp (common printing) to 5,000+ gp (illuminated, ancient)
Traits: Religious value. Illuminated manuscripts are prized art objects.
}}

## Musical Instruments

{{CoreHanging
**Lute.** A stringed instrument, usually with 6–10 strings, carried by traveling bards and entertainers.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 35 gp (common) / 150 gp (quality) / 500+ gp (masterwork)
Traits: Skilled play entertains at inns and courts; mechanical bonuses depend on Perform skill and system.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Drum (Hand).** A small drum, beaten with hand or stick for rhythm.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 3 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 15 gp
Traits: Common across cultures. Military versions used for signaling.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Flute / Pipes.** A wooden flute or panpipes.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0.5 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 gp (wood) / 30 gp (metal)
Traits: Portable; popular with travelers and shepherds alike.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Harp (Small Lap).** A small triangular harp for single-player performance.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 5 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 100 gp (common) / 1,000 gp (masterwork)
Traits: Prestigious instrument; favored in courts.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Horn / Trumpet.** A brass horn; used musically, ceremonially, or as a battlefield signal.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 30 gp
Traits: Carries far; audible 1,000+ feet.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Bagpipes.** A reed instrument with a bag of pressured air. Regional favorite; loud.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 6 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 50 gp
Traits: Distinctive, loud, polarizing. Used as military signaling in some traditions.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Hurdy-Gurdy.** A crank-driven stringed instrument.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 8 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 80 gp
Traits: Distinctive sound; folk and itinerant musician use.
}}

## Recreation and Games

{{CoreHanging
**Playing Cards.** A deck of illustrated cards for games.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 1 gp (common) / 10 gp (ornate)
Traits: Universally recognizable. Gambling implications vary.
}}

\page

{{CoreHanging
**Dice (Set of 5).** A set of bone, wood, or stone dice.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 0 · Bulk: 0
Cost: 5 sp (wood) / 2 gp (bone) / 15 gp (fine carved)
Traits: Universal gambling tool.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Chess / Tafl Set.** A strategic board game, setting-specific.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 10 gp (common) / 200 gp (fine set)
Traits: Associated with nobility and scholars. A mental-exercise and social tool.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Toy (Child's).** A wooden toy, doll, or game for children.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 1 sp to 10 gp depending on craftsmanship
}}

## Saddlery and Horse Tack

{{CoreHanging
**Saddle, Riding.** A leather saddle for general riding.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 25 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 10 gp
Traits: Standard riding saddle.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Saddle, War.** A deeper, higher-backed saddle designed to keep a rider seated during combat.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 30 lbs · Bulk: 3
Cost: 60 gp
Traits: Slight bonus to rider's Riding check in combat.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Saddle, Pack.** A flat saddle for carrying cargo rather than a rider.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 20 lbs · Bulk: 2
Cost: 5 gp
Traits: Increases mount's carry capacity. Cannot be ridden.
}}

\column

{{CoreHanging
**Bridle and Bit.** The headgear and mouthpiece for steering a horse.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 2 lbs · Bulk: 0
Cost: 2 gp
Traits: Essential. Different bit styles suit different horses and riding styles.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Feed Bag / Nosebag.** A canvas bag attached to the horse's head for feeding on the road.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 1 lb · Bulk: 0
Cost: 2 sp
Traits: Allows feeding without dismounting or unhitching.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Horseshoes (Set of 4, unmounted).** Iron shoes for re-shoeing a horse.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Weight: 4 lbs · Bulk: 1
Cost: 1 gp
Traits: Requires a farrier or smith to fit. Portable spares extend a horse's working life on long journeys.
}}

## Toiletries and Personal Care

{{CoreHanging
**Soap.** A bar of soap for personal cleaning.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 5 sp per bar
Traits: Hard soap lasts weeks; soft soap is cheaper but messier.
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Razor and Strop.** A straight razor and leather strop for shaving.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 3 gp
Traits: Can be used as an improvised weapon (Glancing damage, Concealable).
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Comb.** A simple bone or wooden comb.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 2 sp
}}

{{CoreHanging
**Perfume (Small Vial).** A small flask of scented oils or alcohols.
}}
{{CoreMetadata
Cost: 20 gp (common) to 500+ gp (courtly)
Traits: Social tool. Can mask scents (Slight bonus vs. tracking by scent for 1 hour).
}}

\page
